- Source: `include/utils/geometric_distances.h`:97

#### `bool flush(std::chrono::microseconds timeout=std::chrono::duration_cast< std::chrono::microseconds >(std::chrono::seconds(5))) noexcept`
- Source: `include/utils/tracing.h`:484
- Brief: n/a
- Parameters:
  - `timeout` (std::chrono::microseconds): Maximum time to wait for the flush to complete. Defaults to 5 seconds.
- Return: true if all pending spans were exported within the timeout, false if the timeout expired or no provider is active.
- Details: Flush any buffered or in-flight spans to the exporter. Calls ForceFlush() on the underlying SDK TracerProvider so that all spans queued in the processor's internal buffer are exported before the call returns (subject to timeout). When THEMIS_ENABLE_TRACING is not defined this is a no-op and always returns true. timeout Maximum time to wait for the flush to complete. Defaults to 5 seconds. true if all pending spans were exported within the timeout, false if the timeout expired or no provider is active.

#### `bool initialize(const std::string &serviceName, const std::string &endpoint)`
- Source: `include/utils/tracing.h`:206
- Brief: Initialize the global tracer with OTLP HTTP exporter.
- Parameters:

- Return: true if initialization succeeded and backend is reachable; false if backend unreachable (no-op mode, fail-open)
- Details: Initialize. Probes the collector endpoint (3-second timeout) before registering the OTLP exporter. If the probe fails the tracer is marked initialized but operates in no-op mode (every span is discarded), bounding latency impact. serviceName Name of this service (e.g., "themis-server") endpoint OTLP HTTP endpoint (e.g., "http://localhost:4318") true if initialization succeeded and backend is reachable; false if backend unreachable (no-op mode, fail-open) @error_contract Condition ErrorCode Severity Logging Recovery Collector DNS resolution fails TRACE_EXPORT_FAILED (9031) Warning host, port, error Return false; use no-op spans (fail-open) Collector TCP connect fails (timeout 3 s) TRACE_EXPORT_FAILED (9031) Warning host, port, error Return false; use no-op spans (fail-open) Probe throws unexpected exception TRACE_EXPORT_FAILED (9031) Warning error Return false; use no-op spans (fail-open) @degradation fail-open – backend unavailable produces no-op spans; latency bounded to 3 s probe ErrorCode 9030-9039 for tracing error taxonomy serviceName Input parameter. endpoint Input parameter. True when the operation succeeds. Calls: defined(), THEMIS_WARN(), re(), std::regex_search(), str(), size(), std::stoi(), parse_host_port().

#### `Span startChildSpan(const std::string &name, const Span &parent)`
- Source: `include/utils/tracing.h`:370
- Brief: Start Child Span.
- Parameters:
  - `name` (const std::string &): Input parameter.
  - `parent` (const Span &): Input parameter.
- Return: Span RAII object; span ends when object is destroyed
- Details: Start a new span as a child of the given parent span Creates a child span explicitly linked to a parent span. Useful when the parent is not the active context. Phase 2.10: Comprehensive error contract documentation. name Span name for display in tracing backend parent Parent span to link this span to Span RAII object; span ends when object is destroyed @error_contract Phase 2.3 Error Codes (7300-7309, 7362): ERR_TRACING_DEGRADED (7362): Span creation fails Recovery: Returns invalid span (valid_ = false); no-op operations Severity: WARNING User Action: Check tracing backend availability Child Span Semantics: Creates explicit parent-child relationship in trace tree Parent span can be on different thread/context Parent validity is not checked; invalid parent creates orphan span Child inherits parent's trace context and baggage @bounded_resources Span hierarchy depth: limited by tracing backend Resource check: if span tree exceeds depth limit, returns invalid span @thread_safety NOT thread-safe; call from trace context thread @performance O(1) amortized; depends on trace tree depth startSpan() for automatic parent detection startSpanFromHeaders() for distributed tracing name Input parameter. parent Input parameter. Return value. Calls: defined(), getTracer(), Span(), lk(), shouldSample(), StartSpan(), empty(), Baggage::set().

#### `Span startSpan(const std::string &name)`
- Source: `include/utils/tracing.h`:333
- Brief: Start Span.
- Parameters:
  - `name` (const std::string &): Input parameter.
- Return: Span RAII object; span ends when object is destroyed
- Details: Start a new span with the given name Creates a child span of the currently active span (if any). Phase 2.10: Comprehensive error contract documentation. name Span name for display in tracing backend Span RAII object; span ends when object is destroyed @error_contract Phase 2.3 Error Codes (7300-7309, 7362): ERR_TRACING_DEGRADED (7362): Span creation fails Recovery: Returns invalid span (valid_ = false); no-op operations Severity: WARNING User Action: Check tracing backend availability Span Creation Semantics: Span becomes child of active context (if set) Span lifetime bound to returned object (RAII) Tracing disabled: returns empty span (valid_ = false) Span name is immutable; cannot be changed after creation @bounded_resources Active spans limited by tracing backend configuration Resource check: if span count exceeds limit, returns invalid span @thread_safety NOT thread-safe; call from trace context thread @performance O(1) amortized; O(n) on backend when span count exceeds threshold startChildSpan() to explicitly specify parent startSpanFromHeaders() for distributed tracing Span::isValid() to check if creation succeeded name Input parameter. Return value. Calls: defined(), lk(), shouldSample(), Span(), getTracer(), StartSpan(), empty(), Baggage::set().

#### `Span startSpanFromHeaders(const std::string &name, const std::map< std::string, std::string > &headers)`
- Source: `include/utils/tracing.h`:430
- Brief: n/a
- Parameters:
  - `name` (const std::string &): Span name
  - `headers` (const std::map< std::string, std::string > &): HTTP request headers (case-insensitive key lookup)
- Return: Span RAII object; span ends when object is destroyed
- Details: Start a new root span using W3C TraceContext headers for context propagation Extracts the traceparent (and optionally tracestate) header from the provided header map and creates a span that is a child of the upstream trace context. This enables distributed tracing across service boundaries: callers (API gateways, service meshes, SDKs) can propagate their trace IDs into ThemisDB so that all internal spans appear in the same distributed trace. Format of traceparent (W3C Trace Context Level 1): version-traceid-parentid-flags e.g. "00-4bf92f3577b34da6a3ce929d0e0e4736-00f067aa0ba902b7-01" When THEMIS_ENABLE_TRACING is not defined, this falls back to startSpan() and records the raw traceparent value as an attribute for log correlation. Phase 2.10: Comprehensive error contract documentation. name Span name headers HTTP request headers (case-insensitive key lookup) Span RAII object; span ends when object is destroyed @error_contract Phase 2.3 Error Codes (7300-7309, 7362): ERR_TRACING_DEGRADED (7362): W3C header parsing fails Recovery: Falls back to startSpan() without distributed trace context Severity: WARNING User Action: Validate traceparent header format ERR_TRACING_DEGRADED (7362): Span creation fails after parsing Recovery: Returns invalid span (valid_ = false) Severity: WARNING User Action: Check tracing backend availability W3C Trace Context Semantics: Parses traceparent header using W3C Trace Context Level 1 format Optional tracestate header preserved for backend baggage If headers missing: creates root span (new trace ID) Invalid header format: logged as warning; root span created Distributed Trace Propagation: Enables end-to-end tracing across service boundaries Incoming trace ID is preserved in resulting span All child spans inherit propagated trace context Useful for API gateway → ThemisDB → backend service chains @bounded_resources Header parsing: O(header_count) for case-insensitive lookup Resource check: no additional resources required @thread_safety NOT thread-safe; call from trace context thread @performance O(header_count) for header parsing; O(1) for span creation startSpan() for basic span creation without distributed context startChildSpan() for explicit parent linking https://www.w3.org/TR/trace-context/ for W3C Trace Context specification

Phase 2.10: Comprehensive error contract documentation. errorMessage Human-readable error description void @error_contract Phase 2.3 Error Codes (7300-7309, 7362): ERR_TRACING_DEGRADED (7362): Error logging fails (non-fatal) Recovery: Error is logged locally; span continues Severity: WARNING User Action: Check tracing backend availability Span Error Semantics: Error events are aggregated in span attributes Errors do NOT automatically end span; span continues until end() or destructor Multiple errors can be recorded per span Tracing disabled: error call is no-op (valid_ = false) @thread_safety NOT thread-safe; one span per thread/context @performance O(1) for local error logging; depends on tracing backend setStatus() to mark span with error status code ErrorCode::ERR_TRACING_DEGRADED for tracing failures errorMessage Input parameter. Calls: defined(), themis::security::PIIRedactionPolicy::get(), redactForLog(), AddEvent(), SetStatus(), empty(), Baggage::set().

### themis::hash

#### `std::string bytes_to_hex(const unsigned char *data, std::size_t n)`
- Source: `include/utils/hash_util.h`:150
- Brief: Encode n raw bytes as a lowercase hexadecimal string.
- Parameters:
  - `data` (const unsigned char *): Pointer to the raw bytes.
  - `n` (std::size_t): Number of bytes to encode.
- Return: Lowercase hex string of length 2*n.
- Details: Intended for encoding SHA-256 (32 bytes → 64 chars) and similar digests. Uses a nibble-lookup table; avoids the overhead of std::ostringstream. data Pointer to the raw bytes. n Number of bytes to encode. Lowercase hex string of length 2*n.

### themis::observability::detail

#### `bool gmtime_utc(std::time_t tt, std::tm &result) noexcept`
- Source: `include/utils/field_diagnostics_schema.h`:45
- Brief: Thread-safe UTC breakdown of a time_t value.
- Parameters:
  - `tt` (std::time_t): Input POSIX timestamp.
  - `result` (std::tm &): Output std::tm structure filled in UTC.
- Return: true on success; false if the platform call fails.
- Details: std::gmtime() returns a pointer to static storage that is not protected by any lock, making it unsafe to call concurrently. This wrapper uses the POSIX gmtime_r() on non-Windows platforms, and the Windows-specific gmtime_s() on MSVC/MinGW. tt Input POSIX timestamp. result Output std::tm structure filled in UTC. true on success; false if the platform call fails.

#### `std::time_t mktime_utc(std::tm &tm) noexcept`
- Source: `include/utils/field_diagnostics_schema.h`:64
- Brief: Inverse of gmtime_utc(): convert a UTC std::tm to time_t.
- Parameters:
  - `tm` (std::tm &): UTC-based std::tm structure.
- Return: Corresponding POSIX time_t, or (time_t)-1 on failure.
- Details: std::mktime() interprets its argument as local time, which produces incorrect results for UTC timestamps when the host timezone differs from UTC. This wrapper uses POSIX timegm() on non-Windows platforms and the Windows-specific _mkgmtime() on MSVC/MinGW. tm UTC-based std::tm structure. Corresponding POSIX time_t, or (time_t)-1 on failure.

### themis::test

#### `bool poll_until(const std::function< bool()> &predicate, std::chrono::steady_clock::duration timeout=std::chrono::seconds(5))`
- Source: `tests/utils/test_stability.h`:108
- Brief: Deadline-bounded poll for an arbitrary predicate.
- Parameters:
  - `predicate` (const std::function< bool()> &): Callable returning bool; must be side-effect-free in terms of test state (can be called multiple times).
  - `timeout` (std::chrono::steady_clock::duration): Maximum time to wait. A value of zero means a single evaluation with no waiting — the predicate is called exactly once and the result is returned immediately.
- Return: true if predicate returned true before the deadline.
- Details: Calls predicate repeatedly (with yield between calls) until either the predicate returns true or the timeout elapses. Replaces fixed-duration sleep_for() calls that guard asynchronous side-effects. predicate Callable returning bool; must be side-effect-free in terms of test state (can be called multiple times). timeout Maximum time to wait. A value of zero means a single evaluation with no waiting — the predicate is called exactly once and the result is returned immediately. true if predicate returned true before the deadline. false if the deadline elapsed without the predicate becoming true.

#### `void wait_for_clock_advance_ms()`
- Source: `tests/utils/test_stability.h`:77
- Brief: Spin-wait until the system_clock millisecond counter advances by ≥1.
- Parameters: none
- Details: Call this function when the code under test derives a unique identifier or ordering guarantee from the current wall-clock millisecond (e.g. a snapshot ID generated as duration_cast<milliseconds>(now()).count()). Using this helper instead of sleep_for(N ms) eliminates the blind delay: on fast machines the spin completes in < 1 ms; on slow CI agents it waits exactly as long as needed. The function has a built-in safety timeout of 500 ms. If the OS clock fails to advance within that window (highly unusual), the function returns without a guarantee — the calling test will then naturally fail or pass on its own assertions.

#### `void loadPersistedState()`

- Brief: Load Persisted State.
- Parameters: none
- Details: Load persisted schedule/count state from state_db_ into in-memory maps. Called once in the constructor when state_db_ is non-null. Calls: start_key(), back(), lock(), iterateRange(), size(), shard_id(), substr(), nlohmann::json::parse().

#### `void persistState(const std::string &shard_id, int64_t timestamp, uint64_t doc_count)`
- Source: `include/utils/capability_auto_generator.h`:234
- Brief: Persist State.
- Parameters:
  - `shard_id` (const std::string &): Identifier of the shard.
  - `timestamp` (int64_t): Input parameter.
  - `doc_count` (uint64_t): Input parameter.
- Details: Persist the last-run timestamp and document count for a shard. Updates both the in-memory maps (used by the schedule gate and shouldUpdate) and the durable RocksDB state key "utils_capgen_state:<shard_id>". No-op on the RocksDB side when state_db_ is null. This method is public so that callers can pre-seed or reset persisted state (e.g. during migration, testing, or manual override). shard_id Shard identifier timestamp Unix epoch seconds to record as last run time doc_count Document count to record as last known count shard_id Identifier of the shard. timestamp Input parameter. doc_count Input parameter. Calls: lock(), put(), dump().

  - `timeout` (std::chrono::milliseconds): Maximum time the caller waits for the join to complete.

#### `Result< std::vector< uint8_t > > lz4_compress_safe(const uint8_t *data, size_t size, int acceleration=lz4_compression::DEFAULT_ACCELERATION)`
- Source: `src/utils/lz4_codec.cpp`:33
- Brief: Compress size bytes at data with LZ4 (safe version with parameter validation).
- Parameters:
  - `data` (const uint8_t *): Input parameter.
  - `size` (size_t): Input parameter.
  - `acceleration` (int): Input parameter.
- Return: Ok(compressed_bytes) on success; Err on failure or parameter validation failure.
- Details: Lz4 compress safe. data Pointer to input bytes (must not be nullptr unless size==0). size Number of bytes to compress (must not exceed MAX_INPUT_SIZE). acceleration LZ4 acceleration factor (1-1000; values outside range are auto-clamped). Ok(compressed_bytes) on success; Err on failure or parameter validation failure. Parameter Validation: Acceleration parameter is silently clamped to valid range [1-1000]. Invalid values are logged as WARN but do not prevent compression. Max Input: Input size is validated against MAX_INPUT_SIZE to prevent DoS allocation. Output Bounds: Output size is checked against MAX_COMPRESSION_RATIO to detect anomalies. Block-Size Overflow: LZ4 block size limits are validated to prevent overflow (Phase 2.4b). Thread-Safe: Safe for concurrent calls from multiple threads. Acceleration: Higher acceleration = faster compression + lower compression ratio. @error COMPRESSION_FAILED LZ4 compression failed (insufficient memory or state error). @error COMPRESSION_INPUT_INVALID Input size exceeds limit or block-size would overflow. @error CODEC_NOT_SUPPORTED LZ4 library not available (THEMIS_HAS_LZ4 not defined). Phase 2.4b Hardening: Output buffer sizing, block-size overflow check, unavailable handling. data Input parameter. size Input parameter. acceleration Input parameter. Return value. Calls: Ok(), THEMIS_WARN(), fmt::format(), LZ4_compressBound(), resize(), LZ4_compress_fast(), data(), std::string().

#### `Result< std::vector< uint8_t > > zstd_compress_safe(const uint8_t *data, size_t size, int level=3)`
- Source: `src/utils/zstd_codec.cpp`:35
- Brief: Safely compress data using ZSTD with parameter validation.
- Parameters:
  - `data` (const uint8_t *): Input parameter.
  - `size` (size_t): Input parameter.
  - `level` (int): Input parameter.
- Return: Result containing compressed data or error description.
- Details: Zstd compress safe. data Input buffer pointer (must not be nullptr). size Size in bytes (must not exceed compression::MAX_INPUT_SIZE). level Compression level (1-22); values outside range are auto-clamped to [1-22]. Result containing compressed data or error description. Parameter Validation: Compression level is silently clamped to valid range [1-22]. Invalid levels are logged as WARN but do not cause compression to fail. Max Input: Enforces maximum input size to prevent DoS via oversized allocations. Thread-Safe: Uses thread-local ZSTD_CCtx for safe concurrent use (Phase 2.4a). Output Bounds: Enforces MAX_OUTPUT_SIZE and MAX_COMPRESSION_RATIO to prevent buffer overflow. @error COMPRESSION_FAILED ZSTD compression failed (insufficient memory or corrupt state). @error COMPRESSION_INPUT_INVALID Input size exceeds limit or exceeds compression ratio bounds. @error CODEC_NOT_SUPPORTED ZSTD library not available (THEMIS_HAS_ZSTD not defined). data Input parameter. size Input parameter. level Input parameter. Return value. Calls: Ok(), THEMIS_WARN(), THEMIS_ERROR(), fmt::format(), ZSTD_compressBound(), reserve(), resize(), what().

- Brief: Safely decompress ZSTD-compressed data with validation.
- Parameters:
  - `compressed` (const std::vector< uint8_t > &): Input parameter.
- Return: Result containing decompressed data or error description.
- Details: Zstd decompress safe. compressed ZSTD-compressed bytes (output of zstd_compress_safe). Result containing decompressed data or error description. Size Validation: Decompressed size is checked against MAX_DECOMPRESSED_SIZE to prevent decompression bomb attacks (e.g., 1MB compressed -> 4GB decompressed). Checksum Verification: ZSTD frame checksum is validated if present in the frame. Corrupt Data: Malformed or truncated compressed data returns an error, not silent failure. Output Bounds: Enforces MAX_COMPRESSION_RATIO for decompression expansion bounds. @error DECOMPRESSION_FAILED ZSTD decompression failed (invalid or corrupt input). @error COMPRESSION_BOMB_DETECTED Output size exceeds MAX_DECOMPRESSED_SIZE (DoS detected). @error COMPRESSION_RATIO_EXCEEDED Expansion ratio exceeds MAX_COMPRESSION_RATIO. @error CODEC_NOT_SUPPORTED ZSTD library not available (THEMIS_HAS_ZSTD not defined). Phase 2.4a Hardening: Corrupt data detection and decompression bomb prevention. compressed Input parameter. Return value. Calls: empty(), Ok(), size(), THEMIS_ERROR(), ZSTD_getFrameContentSize(), data(), std::string(), logErrorWithContext().

#### `void flush()`

- Brief: Flush audit log to disk.
- Parameters: none
- Return: void (see @error_contract below)
- Details: Flush. Flushes pending audit events to disk. If fsync is enabled in config, ensures data is durably written to storage. void (see @error_contract below) @error_contract If disk full: logs ERR_AUDIT_DISK_FULL and triggers rotation/cleanup If write permission denied: logs ERR_AUDIT_PERMISSION_DENIED If fsync fails: logs ERR_AUDIT_SERVICE_DEGRADED (non-fatal) All failures logged but flush continues with what it can @thread_safety Thread-safe @performance O(pending_events); blocks until flush complete if fsync enabled Resource Limits (Phase 2.6): Queue Depth: Default 10,000 pending events max; older events dropped if exceeded Event Size: Individual event JSON limited to 64 KB; larger events truncated Memory: Bounded queue allocated on initialization; no unbounded growth Disk I/O: Batched writes every 100 events or 100ms (configurable) Critical for audit durability; should be called periodically ErrorCode for diagnostics Calls: lock().

#### `void logEvent(const nlohmann::json &event)`
- Source: `include/utils/audit_logger.h`:280

- Parameters:

- Return: void (failure is logged, not signaled - see @error_contract below)
- Details: Log Event. Logs an event to the audit trail. If hash chaining is enabled, the event is cryptographically linked to the previous event. If encryption is enabled, the event is encrypted before writing. event JSON object containing event data void (failure is logged, not signaled - see @error_contract below) @error_contract Condition ErrorCode Severity Logging Recovery Log file cannot be opened (backend unavailable) AUDIT_PERSISTENCE_FAILED (9012) Critical path + OS error Throw (fail-closed) Write to file fails (I/O error, disk full) AUDIT_WRITE_FAILED (9011) Critical path + bytes attempted Throw (fail-closed) Encryption of event fails (key unavailable) AUDIT_ENCRYPTION_FAILED (9015) Critical key_id Throw (fail-closed) Log rotation fails AUDIT_ROTATION_FAILED (9013) Error log_path + rotated count Continue, skip rotation @degradation fail-closed – backend unavailable propagates as exception; no silent drop @bounded_resources Buffer capacity: cfg.max_buffer_size (default: 1GB) Event size: Individual events capped at 10MB Queue depth: max cfg.max_queued_events entries @thread_safety Thread-safe via internal mutex (file_mu_) @performance O(n) where n is event JSON size; async batch writes if configured ErrorCode 9010-9019 for audit error taxonomy logSecurityEvent() for security-specific event logging event Input parameter. Calls: dump(), size(), err_ctx(), logErrorContext(), substr(), std::chrono::system_clock::now(), time_since_epoch(), count().

#### `std::shared_ptr< grpc::Channel > acquireChannel(const std::string &target, std::shared_ptr< grpc::ChannelCredentials > credentials=nullptr)`
- Source: `include/utils/grpc_channel_pool.h`:110
- Brief: Acquire a channel for the given target with graceful degradation support.
- Parameters:
  - `target` (const std::string &): Input parameter.
  - `credentials` (std::shared_ptr< grpc::ChannelCredentials >): Input parameter.
- Return: Shared pointer to gRPC channel, or nullptr if acquisition failed
- Throws:
  - std::runtime_error: if an error occurs.
- Details: Acquire Channel. Attempts to acquire a channel from the pool. If all channels are in use, waits up to acquire_timeout for one to become available. On timeout or pool exhaustion, returns nullptr and increments acquire_timeout counter. Graceful degradation strategy: If pool is full and timeout expires: caller must handle nullptr If connection cannot be established: channel is not pooled Caller should implement retry logic or fallback strategy target Target address (e.g., "localhost:50051") credentials Channel credentials. A null pointer is rejected by default; set Config::allow_insecure=true for an explicit local/test override. Shared pointer to gRPC channel, or nullptr if acquisition failed @error_contract Condition Behavior Recovery Pool exhausted Returns nullptr Implement backoff/fallback Acquire timeout Returns nullptr Retry later or use degraded mode Connection failed Returns nullptr Check network/endpoint health Shutdown in progress Returns nullptr Wait for shutdown to complete Resource Limits (Phase 2.6): Max Channels per Target: Default 10 (configured by Config.max_channels_per_target) Idle Timeout: 30 seconds (channels closed if idle longer) Acquire Timeout: 10 seconds (max wait time for available channel) Max Concurrent Streams: 100 per channel (gRPC HTTP/2 limit) Connection Pool: Per-target; no global channel limit (unlimited targets) Operator Impact: Monitor acquire_timeouts metric; high values indicate pool undersizing or backend overload. Increase max_channels_per_target if needed. target Input parameter. credentials Input parameter. Return value. std::runtime_error if an error occurs. Calls: load(), getOrCreateTargetPool(), lock(), std::chrono::steady_clock::now(), empty(), front(), pop(), isStale().

#### `void reportFailure(const std::string &target, uint16_t error_code=0)`
- Source: `include/utils/grpc_channel_pool.h`:204
- Brief: Report a failed RPC call for the circuit breaker with optional error details.
- Parameters:
  - `target` (const std::string &): Input parameter.
  - `error_code` (uint16_t): Input parameter.
- Details: Report Failure. Call this after every failed RPC. It increments the failure counter and may trip the circuit (CLOSED → OPEN) when the threshold is reached. Graceful degradation: When circuit is OPEN, new requests are rejected immediately After CB_OPEN_TIMEOUT, circuit transitions to HALF_OPEN for recovery probe Operator can monitor circuit states via getCircuitState() target The endpoint that failed error_code Optional error code for diagnostics (9075-9076 range) target Input parameter. error_code Input parameter. Calls: lk(), std::chrono::steady_clock::now().

- Brief: Report a successful RPC call for the circuit breaker.
- Parameters:
  - `target` (const std::string &): Input parameter.
- Details: Report Success. Call this after every successful RPC. It decrements the failure counter and may transition HALF_OPEN → CLOSED. target Input parameter. Calls: lk().

- Source: `include/utils/hkdf_cache.h`:118
- Brief: Construct an HKDF cache with specified configuration.

- Details: Creates a new cache instance with the given configuration. The cache is divided into 16 shards to reduce lock contention in multi-threaded scenarios. Each shard independently manages LRU eviction. cfg Cache configuration (capacity per shard, TTL) HKDFCacheConfig Thread-safe: Multiple threads can safely construct independent instances Do not share a cache instance across threads without external synchronization, unless using threadLocal() which returns a thread-local singleton

#### `void purge_by_ikm_hash(const std::string &ikm_hash)`
- Source: `include/utils/hkdf_cache.h`:293
- Brief: Purge all entries derived from a specific Input Key Material (IKM).
- Parameters:
  - `ikm_hash` (const std::string &): Input parameter.
- Return: Number of entries purged
- Details: Purge by ikm hash. Enables selective cache invalidation when a root key (IKM) is rotated. For example, after rotating a master encryption key, all keys derived from it can be invalidated via this method, forcing re-derivation with the new master key. Implementation: Scans all cache shards for entries whose IKM matches the given hash Securely wipes matching entries using OPENSSL_cleanse Updates eviction counters ikm_hash SHA-256 hash of the IKM bytes, as hex string (lowercase) Example: "abc123def456..." Number of entries purged @thread_safe Yes - internally synchronized across all shards Performance: O(n) in worst case; consider calling during maintenance window Usage Scenario: //Masterkeyrotationworkflow std::vector<uint8_t>old_master=...; std::stringold_master_hash=sha256_hex(old_master); cache.purge_by_ikm_hash(old_master_hash); //Newkeyswillnowbederivedfromnewmaster autonew_key=cache.derive_cached(new_master,salt,info,32); stats() for cache efficiency monitoring ikm_hash Input parameter. Calls: lk(), begin(), end(), find(), size(), Impl::sha256_hex(), data(), std::next().

#### `std::future< HTTPResponse > get(const std::string &url, const std::unordered_map< std::string, std::string > &headers={})`
- Source: `include/utils/http_client_pool.h`:206
- Brief: Execute GET request using pooled connection with graceful degradation.
- Parameters:
  - `url` (const std::string &): Target URL
  - `headers` (const std::unordered_map< std::string, std::string > &): Additional HTTP headers
- Return: Future with HTTP response (status_code=0 on acquisition failure)
- Details: Attempts to execute GET request using a pooled connection. If the pool is exhausted or connection acquisition times out, the returned future will contain a response with status_code = 0 and body describing the error. Graceful degradation strategy: If pool is full and acquire_timeout expires: returns connection error response If network is unreachable: connection fails and is not re-pooled If server is slow: request_timeout may fire before response arrives Caller should implement retry logic with exponential backoff url Target URL headers Additional HTTP headers Future with HTTP response (status_code=0 on acquisition failure) @error_contract Condition Response Recovery Pool exhausted status_code=0, body="POOL_EXHAUSTED" Wait and retry Acquire timeout status_code=0, body="ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT" Use fallback service Connection timeout status_code=0, body="CONNECT_TIMEOUT" Check endpoint health Request timeout status_code=504, body="Gateway Timeout" Implement retry loop Resource Limits (Phase 2.6): Max Connections: Default 50 (configured by Config.max_connections) Acquire Timeout: 10 seconds (max wait for available connection) Connect Timeout: 5 seconds (max time for TCP handshake) Request Timeout: 30 seconds (max time for complete request/response) Idle Timeout: 30 seconds (connections closed if idle longer) I/O Threads: 4 (configured by Config.io_threads) Lock Stripes: 8 (reduces contention; higher = better concurrency) Operator Impact: Monitor acquire_timeouts metric; high values indicate pool undersizing. Increase max_connections if needed.

#### `std::string getCurrentLEK()`
- Source: `include/utils/lek_manager.h`:175
- Brief: Get current LEK (creates if not exists).

- Return: LEK key_id suitable for use with FieldEncryption::encryptField() Format: "lek_<YYYY-MM-DD>"
- Throws:
  - std::runtime_error: if LEK generation or storage fails
- Details: Get Current LEK. Returns the key ID for today's Log Encryption Key. If today's LEK does not exist, it is automatically generated, encrypted, and stored. This is the primary API for obtaining a key for new log encryption. LEK key_id suitable for use with FieldEncryption::encryptField() Format: "lek_<YYYY-MM-DD>" std::runtime_error if LEK generation or storage fails @thread_safe Yes - internally synchronized Automatic generation: If no LEK exists for today, one is created Idempotent: Calling multiple times returns the same key_id Usage: std::stringlek_id=lek_mgr->getCurrentLEK(); field_enc.encryptField("user_email","alice@example.com",lek_id); Return value. Calls: lk(), getCurrentDateString(), find(), end(), ensureLEKExists(), lekKeyId().

#### `std::string getLEKForDate(const std::string &date_str)`
- Source: `include/utils/lek_manager.h`:205
- Brief: Get LEK for specific date (for decrypting old logs).
- Parameters:
  - `date_str` (const std::string &): Input parameter.
- Return: LEK key_id if found and not revoked; empty string if key not found or revoked
- Throws:
  - std::runtime_error: if key store (RocksDB) is unavailable
- Details: Get LEKFor Date. Retrieves the key ID for a historically-dated LEK. Useful when decrypting log messages created on a previous date. If the key has been revoked, returns empty string. date_str Date of desired LEK in format "YYYY-MM-DD" LEK key_id if found and not revoked; empty string if key not found or revoked std::runtime_error if key store (RocksDB) is unavailable @error_contract Condition ErrorCode Severity Logging Recovery Key store (RocksDB) unavailable – cannot persist new LEK CRYPTO_KEY_NOT_FOUND (9053) Critical date_str, db_key Throw (fail-closed) Key has been revoked CRYPTO_KEY_EXPIRED (9052) Info date_str Return empty string @degradation fail-closed on key store unavailability; no plaintext fallback @thread_safe Yes - internally synchronized Usage: std::stringold_lek=lek_mgr->getLEKForDate("2026-08-01"); if(!old_lek.empty()){ autodecrypted=field_enc.decryptField(encrypted_data,old_lek); } date_str Input parameter. Return value. Calls: lk(), find(), end(), ensureLEKExists(), lekKeyId().

#### `void rotate()`
- Source: `include/utils/lek_manager.h`:227
- Brief: Force immediate key rotation (creates new LEK for today).
- Parameters: none
- Throws:
  - std::runtime_error: if LEK creation fails
  - std::runtime_error: if an error occurs.
- Details: Rotate. Triggers immediate key rotation, creating a new LEK for the current date. Used in security incidents or compliance scenarios where a key needs to be replaced outside the normal daily rotation schedule. @thread_safe Yes - internally synchronized std::runtime_error if LEK creation fails Effect: Next getCurrentLEK() returns the new key Audit: Rotation event is logged if AuditLogger is attached Usage: //Securityincident:forcekeyrotation lek_mgr->rotate(); //AllsubsequentencryptionsusenewLEK std::runtime_error if an error occurs. Calls: lk(), getCurrentDateString(), erase(), del(), dbKey(), ensureLEKExists(), lekKeyId().

#### `void setAuditLogger(std::shared_ptr< AuditLogger > logger)`
- Source: `include/utils/lek_manager.h`:249
- Brief: Attach an AuditLogger so rotation events are recorded.
- Parameters:
  - `logger` (std::shared_ptr< AuditLogger >): Input parameter.
- Details: Set Audit Logger. Call before startAutoRotation() if audit-trail integration is required. Thread-safe; may be called at any time. logger AuditLogger instance (may be nullptr to disable auditing). logger Input parameter. Calls: lk(), std::move().

#### `void startAutoRotation(std::chrono::seconds check_interval=std::chrono::seconds(3600), int max_age_days=30)`
- Source: `include/utils/lek_manager.h`:268
- Brief: Start the background auto-rotation worker.
- Parameters:
  - `check_interval` (std::chrono::seconds): Input parameter.
  - `max_age_days` (int): Input parameter.
- Throws:
  - std::invalid_argument: if max_age_days < 1.
  - std::invalid_argument: if an error occurs.
- Details: Start Auto Rotation. The worker wakes up every check_interval and calls getCurrentLEK() to ensure today's key exists. When the calendar date changes past midnight the new LEK is created automatically without operator intervention. Additionally, any cached key whose date exceeds max_age_days is revoked and a KEY_ROTATED audit event is emitted. Calling startAutoRotation() while a worker is already running is a no-op; call stopAutoRotation() first if you need to change parameters. check_interval How often the worker polls (default: 1 hour). max_age_days Maximum key age before forced revocation; must be >= 1 (default: 30 days). std::invalid_argument if max_age_days < 1. check_interval Input parameter. max_age_days Input parameter. std::invalid_argument if an error occurs. Calls: exchange(), lk(), std::thread().

#### `void stopAutoRotation()`
- Source: `include/utils/lek_manager.h`:278
- Brief: Stop the background auto-rotation worker and join the thread.
- Parameters: none
- Details: Stop Auto Rotation. Blocks until the worker thread has exited. Safe to call even if no worker is running. Calls: lk(), notify_all(), joinable(), join(), store().

#### `~LEKManager()`
- Source: `include/utils/lek_manager.h`:152
- Brief: Destructor – stops the auto-rotation thread if running.
- Parameters: none
- Details: Gracefully stops the background key rotation worker thread (if active) and waits for it to exit. All cached keys are preserved (not wiped) to allow final operations if needed. If explicit zeroing is required, call clear() on consumers before destruction. @thread_safe Safe to call from any thread Blocking: Waits for auto-rotation thread to join Exception-safe: Destructor does not throw

#### `std::vector< PIIFinding > detectInText(const std::string &text) const override`
- Source: `include/utils/ner_detection_engine.h`:108
- Brief: Detects PII entities in unstructured text using NER.
- Parameters:
  - `text` (const std::string &): Input text to scan (unstructured, may contain mixed content).
- Return: Vector of PIIFinding objects with detected entity spans.
- Throws:
  - std::runtime_error: If engine is enabled but no model/gazetteers are available.
  - std::invalid_argument: If text is not valid UTF-8 (when validation enabled).
- Details: text Input text to scan (unstructured, may contain mixed content). Vector of PIIFinding objects with detected entity spans. std::runtime_error If engine is enabled but no model/gazetteers are available. std::invalid_argument If text is not valid UTF-8 (when validation enabled). Thread-safe: Uses mutex for model state access. Fail-Closed: Returns empty findings if model_available_ is false (graceful degradation). Model Unavailability: If gazetteers fail to load during initialize(), model_available_ = false; subsequent detectInText() calls return empty findings rather than crashing. Graceful Degradation: If model becomes unavailable at runtime, detection stops safely; applications can fall back to regex-based detection or log warnings. Inference Timeout: Detection is bounded by inference_timeout_ms (default 5000ms). If timeout is exceeded, remaining patterns are skipped; partial results returned. @error PRIVACY_DETECTION_TIMEOUT Inference exceeded time budget (returns empty findings) @error PRIVACY_ENGINE_LOAD_FAILED Model/gazetteers unavailable (fail-closed) @error PRIVACY_UNICODE_ERROR Invalid UTF-8 input (when validation enabled) model_available_ state determines whether detection proceeds. If false, returns {} immediately.

#### `std::pair< nlohmann::json, std::vector< std::string > > pseudonymize(const nlohmann::json &data)`
- Source: `include/utils/pii_pseudonymizer.h`:78
- Brief: Pseudonymize detected PII in JSON object.

- Throws:
  - std::runtime_error: if pseudonymization key is unavailable (fail-closed)

Requires a valid pseudonymization key; if the key is unavailable, the operation fails closed rather than producing plaintext in the output. data Input JSON with potential PII JSON with PII replaced by UUIDs, and list of UUID mappings created std::runtime_error if pseudonymization key is unavailable (fail-closed) @error_contract Condition ErrorCode Severity Logging Recovery Pseudonymization key unavailable (RAND_bytes failure) CRYPTO_KEY_DERIVATION_FAILED (9050) Critical key_id Throw (fail-closed – no plaintext produced) Database unavailable (cannot persist mapping) CRYPTO_KEY_NOT_FOUND (9053) Critical pii_uuid Throw (fail-closed) @degradation fail-closed – key unavailable → reject; never returns unredacted data on error ErrorCode 9050-9059 for crypto error taxonomy ErrorCode 9040-9049 for privacy detection taxonomy

- Source: `include/utils/pii_pseudonymizer.h`:108

- Details: When set, this callback is invoked automatically after every successful erasePII() call so that any cache tier (e.g. AdaptiveQueryCache) holding data tagged with pii_uuid is purged without requiring caller coordination. fn Callable that receives the pii_uuid to be purged from cache. Must be non-throwing; exceptions are logged and suppressed.

#### `bool acquire_with_timeout(double tokens, std::chrono::milliseconds timeout)`
- Source: `include/utils/rate_limiter.h`:127
- Brief: Block until tokens are available and consume them with timeout support.
- Parameters:
  - `tokens` (double): Input parameter.
  - `timeout` (std::chrono::milliseconds): Input parameter.
- Return: true if tokens acquired successfully; false on timeout or invalid request
- Details: Acquire with timeout. Graceful degradation: If tokens > burst_size: returns false immediately (tokens can never accumulate) If timeout expires: returns false and propagates error to caller Caller should implement fallback strategy (queue rejection or degraded mode) tokens Number of tokens to acquire (must be <= burst_size) timeout Maximum time to wait for tokens to become available true if tokens acquired successfully; false on timeout or invalid request @error_contract If tokens <= 0: returns true immediately (no-op); no logging emitted If tokens > burst_size: returns false (error code: 9073 RATELIMIT_EXCEEDED) If timeout exceeded: returns false (error code: 9073 RATELIMIT_EXCEEDED) On successful acquisition: returns true @bounded_resources Maximum wait time: timeout parameter (caller-controlled) Memory: stack-only; no dynamic allocation CPU: minimal while waiting (uses condition_variable) @thread_safety Thread-safe; condition variable handles concurrent waiters @performance O(wait_time); blocks thread until available or timeout @graceful_degradation When rate limit is exceeded: Caller receives false return value Should log incident (rate limit threshold breached) Can choose to: queue for later, reject request, or use degraded service tier Always use timeouts in production to prevent unbounded blocking Ensure tokens <= burst_size to avoid timeout guarantee violation try_acquire() for non-blocking variant (fail-fast) set_rate() to adjust rate dynamically tokens Input parameter. timeout Input parameter. True when the operation succeeds. Calls: lk(), std::chrono::steady_clock::now(), refill_locked(), wait_for_locked(), std::min(), wait_for().

Otherwise, state is unchanged and false is returned. tokens Number of tokens to acquire (default 1.0) true if tokens were available and consumed; false otherwise @error_contract Condition ErrorCode Severity Logging Recovery tokens <= 0 (no-op) n/a n/a None Return true Token bucket exhausted (insufficient tokens) RATELIMIT_EXCEEDED (9073) Warning requested, available, rate_per_s Return false (RATE_LIMIT_EXHAUSTED) @degradation explicit false return + structured diagnostic on every rejection @bounded_resources Tokens capped at burst_size (no unbounded accumulation) Operation O(1) time complexity @thread_safety Thread-safe via internal mutex (no spin-loops) @performance Sub-microsecond; no sleep/wait ErrorCode 9070-9079 for concurrency error taxonomy acquire() for blocking variant with automatic retry tokens Input parameter. True when the operation succeeds. Calls: lk(), refill_locked(), themis::utils::makeErrorContext(), std::to_string(), themis::utils::logErrorWithContext().

#### `std::vector< PIIFinding > detectInText(const std::string &text) const override`
- Source: `include/utils/regex_detection_engine.h`:123
- Brief: Detects PII in input text using compiled regex patterns.
- Parameters:
  - `text` (const std::string &): Input text to scan (must be valid UTF-8 if validation enabled).
- Return: Vector of PIIFinding objects with detected matches.
- Throws:
  - std::invalid_argument: If UTF-8 validation is enabled and text is not valid UTF-8.
  - std::length_error: If text exceeds configured maximum size.
  - std::regex_error: If regex matching fails (malformed pattern or catastrophic backtracking).
- Details: text Input text to scan (must be valid UTF-8 if validation enabled). Vector of PIIFinding objects with detected matches. std::invalid_argument If UTF-8 validation is enabled and text is not valid UTF-8. std::length_error If text exceeds configured maximum size. std::regex_error If regex matching fails (malformed pattern or catastrophic backtracking). Thread-safe: Uses mutex for pattern read access. Timeout: Regex matching is bounded by PATTERN_TIMEOUT_MS per pattern batch. If exceeded, remaining patterns are skipped; partial results are returned. Fail-Closed: Invalid input throws exception rather than returning empty (prevents silent bypass). Backtracking Detection: Malformed patterns with known ReDoS vulnerabilities are pre-filtered. Input Bounds: Text is checked against max_input_size limit (default 10MB). @error PRIVACY_DETECTION_TIMEOUT Regex matching exceeded time budget (partial results returned) @error PRIVACY_PATTERN_OVERFLOW Regex pattern complexity or backtracking limit exceeded @error PRIVACY_INVALID_INPUT UTF-8 validation failed or input oversized @error PRIVACY_ENGINE_FAILED Regex engine threw unexpected exception If timeout occurs, spdlog warning is emitted and detection stops; findings detected so far are returned.

### themis::utils::Serialization::Decoder

#### `Decoder(const std::vector< uint8_t > &data)`
- Source: `include/utils/serialization.h`:164
- Brief: Construct a decoder for the given serialized data.
- Parameters:
  - `data` (const std::vector< uint8_t > &): Serialized data buffer (must remain valid for decoder lifetime).
- Details: data Serialized data buffer (must remain valid for decoder lifetime). Schema Validation: Type tags are validated during decode operations. Mismatches between expected and actual type return error, not silent coercion. Nesting Depth: Tracks recursion depth during array/object decoding. Depth exceeding MAX_NESTING_DEPTH (32) causes deserialization error.

#### `size_t beginArray()`
- Source: `include/utils/serialization.h`:223
- Brief: Begin decoding an array and return its size.
- Parameters: none
- Return: Number of elements in the array.
- Throws:
  - std::length_error: If nesting depth exceeds MAX_NESTING_DEPTH (32).
  - std::runtime_error: If next element is not an array (schema mismatch).
  - std::length_error: if an error occurs.
- Details: Begin Array. Number of elements in the array. std::length_error If nesting depth exceeds MAX_NESTING_DEPTH (32). std::runtime_error If next element is not an array (schema mismatch). Nesting Depth Check (Phase 2.4c): Each array/object nesting increments depth; depth > 32 causes error to prevent stack overflow on crafted input. Return value. std::length_error if an error occurs. Calls: fmt::format(), readTag(), readUInt32().

#### `size_t beginObject()`
- Source: `include/utils/serialization.h`:237
- Brief: Begin decoding an object and return the number of fields.
- Parameters: none
- Return: Number of key-value pairs in the object.
- Throws:
  - std::length_error: If nesting depth exceeds MAX_NESTING_DEPTH (32).
  - std::runtime_error: If next element is not an object (schema mismatch).
  - std::length_error: if an error occurs.
- Details: Begin Object. Number of key-value pairs in the object. std::length_error If nesting depth exceeds MAX_NESTING_DEPTH (32). std::runtime_error If next element is not an object (schema mismatch). Nesting Depth Check (Phase 2.4c): Same depth limit as beginArray(). Schema Validation: Mismatch between expected and actual type causes error. Return value. std::length_error if an error occurs. Calls: fmt::format(), readTag(), readUInt32().

Fail-Safe: Returns empty vector on bounds overflow. Security: Prevents out-of-bounds reads on corrupted serialized data. Return value. Calls: readTag(), readUInt32(), size(), binary(), begin().

Validate with Decoder after encoding. @error_contract Condition ErrorCode Severity Logging Recovery Memory exhaustion while encode* operations append/grow the buffer std::bad_alloc (SERIALIZATION_FAILED 9080) Critical – Propagates exception @bounded_resources Nesting depth is limited by available stack space; callers are responsible for bounding the number of nested begin/end calls. #### `bool submit(std::shared_ptr< Task > task, std::chrono::milliseconds timeout=std::chrono::seconds(5))`
- Source: `include/utils/thread_pool_manager.h`:109
- Brief: Submit a task to the thread pool for execution.

  - `timeout` (std::chrono::milliseconds): Input parameter.
- Return: true if task accepted, false if queue full or timeout exceeded
- Details: Submit. Submits a task to the work queue. The task will be executed by an available worker thread based on priority. If queue is full, returns false after timeout. task Shared pointer to task to execute timeout Maximum time to wait for queue space (default 5 seconds) true if task accepted, false if queue full or timeout exceeded @error_contract Condition ErrorCode Severity Logging Recovery Pool is stopped (not running) THREADPOOL_SHUTDOWN (9071) Error pool_name Return false (THREAD_POOL_OVERLOAD) Queue at capacity, timeout elapsed THREADPOOL_QUEUE_FULL (9070) Error pool_name, queue_size, task_name Return false (THREAD_POOL_OVERLOAD) @degradation explicit false return on any rejection; no silent discard @bounded_resources Queue depth capped at config.queue_size (default: 1,000 tasks) Task wait time bounded by timeout parameter @thread_safety Thread-safe for concurrent submit() calls @performance O(log n) insertion into priority queue where n = queue depth ErrorCode 9070-9079 for concurrency error taxonomy task Input parameter. timeout Input parameter. True when the operation succeeds. Calls: themis::utils::makeErrorContext(), themis::utils::logErrorWithContext(), lock(), wait_for(), size(), std::to_string(), getName(), push().

#### `bool waitAll(std::chrono::milliseconds timeout=std::chrono::seconds(30))`
- Source: `include/utils/thread_pool_manager.h`:131
- Brief: Wait for all submitted tasks to complete.
- Parameters:
  - `timeout` (std::chrono::milliseconds): Input parameter.
- Return: true if all tasks completed; false if timeout exceeded
- Details: Wait All. Blocks until all previously submitted tasks have finished execution or timeout is exceeded. timeout Maximum time to wait (default 30 seconds) true if all tasks completed; false if timeout exceeded @error_contract Returns false if timeout exceeded (logs ERR_THREADPOOL_TIMEOUT) If pool not running: returns false with warning Task execution exceptions: logged but waitAll() returns true (tasks finished) @thread_safety Blocking call; thread-safe for concurrent waitAll() calls @performance O(n) polling where n = number of active tasks Use sparingly in production; consider async patterns instead Set timeout appropriately for your task workload timeout Input parameter. True when the operation succeeds. Calls: std::chrono::steady_clock::now(), lock(), empty(), std::this_thread::sleep_for(), std::chrono::milliseconds().

### utils

#### `std::string generate_uuid_v4()`
- Source: `include/utils/uuid.h`:30
- Brief: Generate a random UUID v4 string.
- Parameters: none
- Return: Lowercase UUID v4 string of the form "xxxxxxxx-xxxx-4xxx-yxxx-xxxxxxxxxxxx".
- Details: Uses a thread-local Mersenne-Twister seeded from std::random_device so that IDs are unique across threads without lock contention. Lowercase UUID v4 string of the form "xxxxxxxx-xxxx-4xxx-yxxx-xxxxxxxxxxxx".

#### `std::string generate_uuid_v7()`
- Source: `include/utils/uuid.h`:85
- Brief: Generate a time-ordered UUID v7 string (RFC 9562).
- Parameters: none
- Return: Lowercase UUID v7 string.
- Details: The UUID encodes the current Unix timestamp in milliseconds in the most-significant 48 bits, followed by the version nibble (7), 12 random bits, the 2-bit variant (0b10), and 62 random bits. UUID v7 values generated within the same process are monotonically increasing: two calls within the same millisecond yield the same timestamp portion, with the random suffix providing uniqueness. Format: "xxxxxxxx-xxxx-7xxx-yxxx-xxxxxxxxxxxx" where the first 48 bits encode unix_ts_ms and y ∈ {8,9,a,b}. Lowercase UUID v7 string.

#### `void startBackgroundJob(std::chrono::seconds interval, std::function< std::vector< std::pair< std::string, std::chrono::system_clock::time_point > >(const std::string &)> entity_provider, std::function< bool(const std::string &)> archive_handler, std::function< bool(const std::string &)> purge_handler)`
- Source: `include/utils/retention_manager.h`:220
- Brief: Start an asynchronous background retention-enforcement job.
- Parameters:
  - `interval` (std::chrono::seconds): How often to run the retention check.
  - `entity_provider` (std::function< std::vector< std::pair< std::string, std::chrono::system_clock::time_point > >(const std::string &)>): Callback that provides entities to check.
  - `archive_handler` (std::function< bool(const std::string &)>): Archive implementation.
  - `purge_handler` (std::function< bool(const std::string &)>): Purge implementation.
- Details: The background thread wakes up every interval, calls entity_provider for each registered policy, and applies archive / purge handlers as appropriate. Call stopBackgroundJob() to stop it. Only one background job may run at a time. Calling startBackgroundJob() while a job is already running is a no-op. interval How often to run the retention check. entity_provider Callback that provides entities to check. archive_handler Archive implementation. purge_handler Purge implementation.

#### `void stopBackgroundJob()`
- Source: `include/utils/retention_manager.h`:233
- Brief: Stop the background retention-enforcement job.
- Parameters: none
- Details: Stop Background Job. Blocks until the current iteration finishes, then joins the thread. Safe to call even if no background job is running. Calls: lk(), notify_all(), joinable(), join(), store().
